| `transitions_test.dart` 'RelativePositionedTransition animates' reads the `Positioned` before the pump, so its second check sees the old values | the Go test reads the widget again and expects the animated values | the Dart check is stale by accident |
| the `Navigator` test of `ticker_provider_test.dart` ('Navigation with TickerMode') | ported in step 10.2 (`TestNavigationWithTickerMode`) | |
| `ScrollPhysics`, `ScrollPosition`, `ScrollActivity`, `ScrollController`, `ScrollBehavior` and `ScrollMetrics` are classes whose methods subclasses override | each is an interface with a `...Base` struct (`ScrollMetricsMixin` for the metrics) that a type embeds and sets up with `InitScrollPhysicsBase(&b, self, parent)`, `InitScrollPositionBase`, `InitScrollActivityBase`, `InitScrollControllerBase`, `InitScrollBehaviorBase`, `InitScrollMetricsMixin`; the base keeps the outer value and calls the overridable methods through it. `NewScrollController`, `NewScrollBehavior` and the concrete activities have `Init...` forms where a subclass is useful (`InitDrivenScrollActivity`, `InitBallisticScrollActivity`, `gestures.InitVerticalDragGestureRecognizer`) | Go has no virtual methods (the interface plus Base convention of this section) |
| `ScrollContext` is implemented by `ScrollableState`; `ScrollPosition` reaches the framework with `WidgetsBinding.instance` and `scheduleMicrotask` | `ScrollContext.StorageContext()` gives the position its `WidgetsBinding`; a microtask is `WidgetsBinding.Post` (run at once when the position has no binding) | no singletons (§13) |

| `ScrollBehavior.pointerAxisModifiers` makes a mouse wheel scroll the other axis while a key (shift) is down | ported: `ScrollBehavior.PointerAxisModifiers()` (a slice, with `ScrollBehaviorCopy.PointerAxisModifiers`) and the keyboard state of the binding; the engine `respond` cases of `scrollable_test.dart` are skipped | the engine is told through `PointerScrollEvent.Respond` only |
| `Scrollable.restorationId` (`RestorationMixin`) and the selection support (`SelectableRegion`) | not ported until the restoration and selection steps; the `restorationId` of the views of step 9.3 (`ScrollView`, `PageView`, `NestedScrollView`, `ListWheelScrollView`, `SingleChildScrollView`) is accepted and has no effect, and `TwoDimensionalScrollable` has none | not in the scrolling step |
| `ScrollBehavior.buildOverscrollIndicator` builds `GlowingOverscrollIndicator` (Android, Fuchsia) or `StretchingOverscrollIndicator` | returns the child on every platform; the indicators are not ported, and the 'default android overscroll indicator' case of `scroll_behavior_test.dart` is skipped | Android-only visuals, after the Material step |
| `Viewport(cacheExtent, cacheExtentStyle)` | only `ScrollCacheExtent`, as in `RenderViewport` (§12.10, step 9.1) | the deprecated pair |

| the frame counts of `scroll_activity_test.dart` 'Scrolling with list view changes, remaining overscrolled' (8) and of `scrollable_test.dart` 'Holding scroll' (3) | the Go tests expect 7 and 2: a spring-back that ends is one frame shorter in Glutter, and no cause was found (the start and tick of the ticker, the begin frame of the scheduler and the spring simulation were compared with Flutter's code) | open follow-up of step 9.2 |
| `scrollable_test.dart`, `scroll_notification_test.dart`, `scrollbar_test.dart`, `scroll_behavior_test.dart` and `scrollable_of_test.dart` scroll a `SingleChildScrollView`, a `ListView` or a `CustomScrollView` | the ports scroll a `Scrollable` with a `Viewport` and one sliver box per child (the test helpers `testList` and `newSliverBox`), so a user scroll also dispatches a `ScrollMetricsNotification` (a `SingleChildScrollView` repaints without laying out again); the widget-based cases that need the lazy lists, `PageView`, `NestedScrollView` or the semantics tester wait for step 9.3 | the scroll views come with step 9.3 |

| `FocusTraversalGroup` creates its node in `initState` and the policy of the widget can be null | the same (a nil `Policy` means `ReadingOrderTraversalPolicy`); `FocusTraversalGroupOpts` has `DescendantsAreNotFocusable` and `DescendantsAreNotTraversable` because the Flutter defaults are true (§12.2). The geometry tests compare `float32` matrix results with a tolerance of 1e-4 (`ui.Matrix4` is `float32`, §12.10 above) | the options rule |
| `Intent`, `Action<T extends Intent>`, `Map<Type, Action<Intent>>` and `Intent.doNothing` | `Intent` is an interface that a value struct satisfies by embedding `IntentBase` (comparable, so it works as a value in a `ShortcutMap`); `Action` is a non-generic interface with `IntentType() reflect.Type` (an interface type accepts every intent that implements it, which stands for Dart's covariance), embedding `ActionBase` (`InitActionBase(&a.ActionBase, a)` gives it the outer value, so the default methods call the overrides); `ActionMap` is `map[reflect.Type]Action` with keys from `IntentTypeOf[T]()`; `CallbackAction[T]` keeps the type parameter; `Intent.doNothing` is `DoNothingIntent{}`. An action listener is removed by the handle that `AddActionListener` returns. In debug builds an action given an intent it can not handle panics with the tag `ActionIntentMismatch` (Dart: a cast error or the "cannot be handled by" assertion) | Go has no covariance or subclassing of generic classes |
| `ActionDispatcher` and `ContextAction<T>` | `ActionDispatcher` is an interface with `ActionDispatcherBase` (`NewActionDispatcher(focusManager...)`, `InitActionDispatcherBase` so that `InvokeActionIfEnabled` calls an overridden `InvokeAction`); `InvokeAction(action, intent, bc)` takes a nil context for "the context of the primary focus". `ContextAction` is the interface `IsEnabledWithContext`/`InvokeWithContext` that `ContextActionBase` implements, and `NextFocusAction`, `PreviousFocusAction`, `DirectionalFocusAction`, `ScrollAction` and `PrioritizedAction` are context actions | Go has no optional parameters |
| `Action<T>.overridable(defaultAction:, context:)` with `_OverridableAction` and `_OverridableContextAction` | `NewOverridableAction(defaultAction, bc)` is one implementation of both; a default action that is a `ContextAction` makes a context action. The recursion guards are debug-only checks with the tag `ActionOverrideRecursion` (Dart: `debugAssertIsEnabledMutuallyRecursive` and the others), an override that finds itself panics with `ActionOverrideSelf`, and a context action invoked without a context with `ActionContextMissing` | one type covers both Dart classes |
| `Map<ShortcutActivator, Intent>` (`Shortcuts.shortcuts`, `ShortcutManager.shortcuts`, `WidgetsApp.shortcuts`) | `ShortcutMap` is an ordered slice of `ShortcutEntry{Activator, Intent}`: the order decides which of the activators that accept an event wins (Dart's maps keep insertion order too), an activator that appears twice keeps the place of its first entry and the intent of its last, and maps are equal when their normalized entries are. `SingleActivator`, `CharacterActivator` and `LogicalKeySet` are comparable values (a `LogicalKeySet` has at most four keys, encoded sorted; its constructors panic with `KeySetDuplicateKey` and `KeySetEmpty`; `SingleActivator` with a modifier as its trigger panics with `SingleActivatorTriggerIsModifier`). `ShortcutActivator.isActivatedBy` (deprecated) is not ported: call `Accepts` | Go maps can not hold interface keys that are not comparable, and need no order |
| `ShortcutManager`, `Shortcuts.manager` and `ShortcutRegistry` | `ShortcutManager` is an interface with `ShortcutManagerBase` (`InitShortcutManagerBase`), `NewShortcutsManager(manager, child)` is `Shortcuts.manager`; `ShortcutsOpts.ExcludeSemantics` is the negation of `includeSemantics`. The menu serialization (`ShortcutSerialization`, `ShortcutManager.serializeForMenu` and the `SingleActivator`/`CharacterActivator` serialization) is not ported: it exists for the platform menu bar, which Glutter does not have yet. `ShortcutRegistry` takes the scheduler for its post-frame notification (`NewShortcutRegistry(binding.Scheduler())`) and its entry errors have the tags `ShortcutRegistryEmpty`, `ShortcutRegistryDuplicate`, `ShortcutRegistryEntryDisposed` and `ShortcutRegistryEntryForeign` | no singletons; the menu bar comes later |
| `DefaultTextEditingShortcuts`, `intentForMacOSSelector` and the text editing intents | `DefaultTextEditingShortcuts` and `DefaultTextEditingShortcutMap()` have only the Linux bindings (common, clipboard, numpad with Num Lock, Home and End), and every target platform uses them (SPEC §11.1: "Linux bindings"); the Apple, Windows and Android maps, the web disabling maps and `intentForMacOSSelector` are not ported. The directional intents are values with constructors (`NewDeleteCharacterIntent(forward)`, `NewExtendSelectionToLineBreakIntent(forward, collapse, opts)`, ...); `collapseSelection` together with `collapseAtReversal` panics with the tag `CaretMovementIntentInvalid`. `ReplaceTextIntent` and `UpdateSelectionIntent` exist since step 11.4. `SelectionChangedCause` is in `services` with `SelectionChangedCauseUnspecified` as its zero value | Glutter is Linux first |
| `WidgetsApp` and `WidgetsApp.router` | (a `TapRegionSurface` is in the tree of both since step 11.4) `NewWidgetsApp(color, opts)` and the generic `NewWidgetsAppRouter[T](color, opts)` (the router configuration type `T` replaces `Router<Object>`); `WidgetsApp.defaultShortcuts` and `defaultActions` are the functions `WidgetsAppDefaultShortcuts()` and `WidgetsAppDefaultActions()` (a new map each call, no mutable global; the web shortcuts are not ported). `LocaleListResolutionCallback` and `LocaleResolutionCallback` return a second `bool` for Dart's null; `PageRouteFactory` is not generic; `LocalizationsResolver` takes the binding (`NewLocalizationsResolver(binding, supported, opts)`) and compares the supported locales by value. The debug assertions and the thrown `FlutterError`s are the tags `WidgetsAppHomeRedundant`, `WidgetsAppHomeAndDefaultRoute`, `WidgetsAppNoRoute`, `WidgetsAppBuilderOnly`, `WidgetsAppPageRouteBuilderMissing`, `WidgetsAppRouterConfigConflict`, `WidgetsAppRouterDelegateMissing`, `WidgetsAppRouterParserMissing`, `WidgetsAppUnknownRouteMissing`, `WidgetsAppUnknownRouteNil`, and a locale that some delegate does not support is reported once with `LocaleNotSupported`. Not part of it yet: `RootRestorationScope` (`restorationScopeId`), `SharedAppData`, `WindowManager`, `PerformanceOverlay`, `SemanticsDebugger`, `WidgetInspector`; the `CheckedModeBanner` is in it since step 12.4 (`WidgetsAppOpts.DebugShowCheckedModeBanner`, an `Opt[bool]` that is true by default and counts only in debug builds; `WidgetsApp.debugAllowBannerOverride` is not ported as it is a global flag); `useInheritedMediaQuery` (deprecated) is dropped. The `Focus` that dismisses the tooltips on Escape is in it since step 11.5 | each is added with the widget it needs |
| `FocusableActionDetector` | `FocusableActionDetectorOpts` names the negated defaults: `Disabled` (`enabled`), `DescendantsAreNotFocusable`, `DescendantsAreNotTraversable` and `ExcludeFocusSemantics` (`includeFocusSemantics`). A highlight callback that would run during the build phase panics with the tag `FocusableActionDetectorCallbackDuringBuild` (Dart: an assertion) | SPEC §12 defaults |

| `TextInput` is a singleton with static methods, and talks to the engine over the `flutter/textinput` channel with JSON (`TextInput.setClient`, `setEditingState`, `TextInputClient.updateEditingState` and the rest); `toJSON`/`fromJSON` of `TextEditingValue`, `TextInputConfiguration`, `AutofillConfiguration` and `TextEditingDelta` | `services.TextInput` is a value that the `WidgetsBinding` owns (`Binding.TextInput()`) and `ui.PlatformDispatcher` is its backend: the platform control (Flutter's `_PlatformTextInputControl`) gives the platform a `ui.TextInputState` (enabled, view, purpose, hints, surrounding text without the composing text, selection, caret rectangle in view coordinates) through `PlatformDispatcher.SetTextInput`, and takes `ui.TextInputEvent`s (delete, commit, preedit, in that order) from `PlatformDispatcher.OnTextInput`, applying them to its copy of the value as Flutter's engines do and giving the client a value or a `TextEditingDelta`. The messages of the channel are methods `HandleUpdateEditingState(clientID, value)`, `HandlePerformAction` and so on; a message for another client id is dropped, and -1 is accepted in a debug build. There is no JSON; `NewTextEditingDeltaFromReplacement` is `TextEditingDelta.fromJSON` without the map | no channel and no singleton (§13); the platform keeps no model of the text |
| `TextEditingValue()` has `selection` collapsed at -1 and `composing` `TextRange.empty` as defaults; `TextInputConfiguration` has non-null defaults; `TextInputAction.unspecified` is a value | `NewTextEditingValue(TextEditingValueOpts)` (the Go zero value has a caret at 0) and `TextEditingValueEmpty`; `NewTextInputConfiguration(opts)` with the negated option names `DisableAutocorrect`, `DisableSuggestions`, `DisableInteractiveSelection` and `DisableIMEPersonalizedLearning`; `TextInputActionUnspecified` is the Go zero value (the default, `done`) and Flutter's `unspecified` is `TextInputActionPlatformDefault`; `TextInputType` is a comparable struct whose zero value is `text`; `TextSelection`, `TextEditingValue`, `TextInputConfiguration` and `AutofillConfiguration` have `Equal` and `CopyWith(XCopy)` | §12.1 and §12.3 |
| `FilteringTextInputFormatter` takes a `Pattern` (a `String` or a `RegExp`); `LengthLimitingTextInputFormatter(int? maxLength)` | the pattern is a `*regexp.Regexp` (RE2 syntax, UTF-8; a `String` pattern is `regexp.MustCompile(regexp.QuoteMeta(s))`), `FilteringTextInputFormatterSingleLine` and `FilteringTextInputFormatterDigitsOnly` are variables; `NewLengthLimitingTextInputFormatter(foundation.Opt[int])`; characters are grapheme clusters from go-text's segmenter; a limit of 0 or below -1 panics in a debug build with `MaxLengthInvalid`; the ported tests with non-ASCII text use byte offsets | Go has no `Pattern`; §12.10 text offsets |
| `TextEditingDelta.fromJSON` counts a deleted character as one UTF-16 unit; `TextLayoutMetrics.isWhitespace(int codeUnit)` | `NewTextEditingDeltaFromReplacement` counts a deleted character as the bytes of one UTF-8 character, so that the last character of the composing region goes whole; `TextLayoutMetricsIsWhitespace(rune)` takes a code point | UTF-8 offsets |
| The text input of a platform can be a `TextInputControl` of the app; `TextInputType.none` asks the platform for no on-screen keyboard | the platform control keeps the input method of the platform off while a control of the app is current and for `TextInputType.none` | on the desktop the input method is the keyboard |
| `ScribbleClient` and `TextInput.registerScribbleElement` (stylus handwriting on iOS); `SystemContextMenuController` (iOS) | not ported | no platform of Glutter has them |
| `AutofillScopeMixin.attach` sends the configurations of all the fields of the scope | `AutofillScopeAttach(scope, textInput, trigger, configuration)` checks that every field enables autofill (`AutofillClientDisabled` in a debug build) and attaches the trigger | no platform of Glutter fills fields |
| Wayland `zwp_text_input_v3` is not part of Flutter (the GTK embedder uses `GtkIMContext`) | `internal/platform/linux/wayland` binds the manager (version 1) when the compositor offers it: `enable`, surrounding text (at most 4000 bytes around the cursor), content type with the preedit-shown hint, cursor rectangle and `commit` follow `SetTextInput`; `done` delivers one `ui.TextInputEvent`; `leave` removes the preedit. The actions (`action`) are not used | §8.6 |
| IBus and Fcitx5 are reached through `GtkIMContext` and its input-method modules (the GTK embedder) | `internal/dbus` talks to them directly (`InputMethod`, `OpenInputMethod`): IBus on the private bus of its daemon (`IBUS_ADDRESS`, or the file `ibus/bus/<machine-id>-<host>-<display>` of the config directory) and, when that bus cannot be reached, through its portal `org.freedesktop.portal.IBus` on the session bus; Fcitx5 on `org.fcitx.Fcitx5` or `org.freedesktop.portal.Fcitx`. The framework named by `XMODIFIERS`, `GTK_IM_MODULE` or `QT_IM_MODULE` goes first; with no name, Fcitx5, then IBus. `ForwardKeyEvent` (IBus) and `ForwardKey` (Fcitx5), the content hints and the Fcitx5 cursor scale are not used. The same code serves X11 and Wayland without `zwp_text_input_v3` | SPEC §8.3 names only `org.freedesktop.IBus`, which a sandboxed app cannot reach; Gio reads the same file |
| `GtkIMContext` gets a key event and answers synchronously | while a text field has the focus every key goes to the input method first, on a goroutine of its own (`internal/platform/linux/ime`), with a limit of one second per call; the keys the input method does not consume are sent on in their order, and the key-up of a consumed key is dropped. On Wayland the key is translated (and the compose state advanced) before the input method answers | the UI thread must not wait for D-Bus (SPEC §13) |

| `RenderEditable` | `rendering.NewRenderEditable(textDirection, startHandleLayerLink, endHandleLayerLink, offset, textSelectionDelegate, opts)`; `maxLines` is `Opt[int]` with `MaxLinesUnlimited` (0) for Dart's `null` (the default of an unset value is 1, which an `Opt` could not tell from `null`), and the engine and the gesture binding are options (`TextEngine`, `Gestures`) because there is no global binding. The floating cursor of iOS, the Apple rules of `getLineAtOffset`-based selection and of the caret height, and the word selection that skips whitespace on iOS and Android are not ported. The asserts are the tags `EditableCursorColorMissing`, `EditableCursorHeightInvalid`, `EditableCursorWidthInvalid`, `EditableExpandsWithLines`, `EditableMaxLinesInvalid`, `EditableMinLinesGreaterThanMaxLines`, `EditableMinLinesInvalid`, `EditableNoTapDown`, `EditableObscuringCharacterInvalid`, `EditableWordOffsetNegative`, `EditableRecognizerNotReadOnly`, `EditableRecognizerUnsupported`, `EditableSemanticsInfoMissing` and `VerticalCaretMovementRunInvalid`; the assert of `_lineNumberFor` (no line for a position) is dropped because no position gets there | the Apple behaviors come with the Apple platforms; the others follow from the API conventions |
| `Paragraph.getLineBoundary`, `getGlyphInfoAt`, `getPositionForOffset` and `getBoxesForRange` at a line break | as Skia does: the line break that ends a line is not part of its `getLineBoundary` range; `getGlyphInfoAt` of the line break is a glyph without width at the end of the text of its line (or at the start of an empty line); `getBoxesForRange` of the line break alone is one box without width there (a range with more text has no box for it); the position at the end of the text of any line but the last is upstream, before the line break, as for a soft break. The caret before a line break and the vertical movement across lines need them | the editable text needs the caret at the end of every line |
| Grapheme clusters of the text | `foundation.GraphemeCount`, `GraphemeOffset` and `GraphemeBoundaries` and the paragraph layout use go-text's segmenter, plus a fix: v0.3.4 ends an emoji ZWJ sequence when another one follows it directly, so a cluster that ends with a ZWJ after a pictograph is joined to the next cluster that starts with a pictograph (rule GB11). The pictographs are an approximation of the Unicode `Extended_Pictographic` property (the emoji blocks and the symbols with an emoji presentation). All the offsets of the text editing are UTF-8 bytes | Dart's `characters` package is not available, and the fix goes when go-text fixes the segmenter |
| `EditableText`, `EditableTextState`, `TextEditingController` | `NewEditableText(controller, focusNode, style, cursorColor, backgroundCursorColor, opts)`; `maxLines` has the default 1 and `MaxLines: Some(0)` is `null`; the options with a default of `true` are negated. `TextEditingController` is a struct with `SetText`, `SetValue`, `SetSelection`, `Clear` and `ClearComposing` (Dart's setters), and `SetBuildTextSpan(f)` replaces Dart's override of `buildTextSpan`. The state implements `TextInputClient`, `TextSelectionDelegate`, `TextLayoutMetrics`'s delegate and `AutofillClient` and has the actions of the text editing intents (movement by character, word, line, paragraph, page and document; deletion; select all; copy, cut and paste through the clipboard; transposing characters; undo and redo through `UndoHistory`). The caret blinks (`cursorOpacityAnimates` does not fade it); the clipboard is read and written through callbacks, so a failure is reported with the tags `EditableTextClipboardFailed` and `EditableTextPasteFailed`. The failures of the constructor are the tags `EditableTextObscuringCharacterInvalid`, `EditableTextMinLinesInvalid`, `EditableTextMinLinesGreaterThanMaxLines`, `EditableTextExpandsWithLines` and `EditableTextObscuredMultiline`; `EditableTextNotBuilt` and `EditableTextNotMounted` guard the render object, `UpdateSelectionOutOfRange` the selection intent and `EditableTextTapOutsideTrackpad` a trackpad tap on a mobile platform; the controller has `TextEditingControllerSelectionInvalid` and `TextEditingControllerComposingInvalid`. Not ported: spell check, the stylus handwriting, Live Text, the processing of text by other apps, the floating cursor, the magnifier, the look up, search web and share buttons, `contentInsertionConfiguration` and the autofill groups beyond the hints | a Go controller cannot be subclassed; the rest follows from the platforms Glutter has |
| The composing text of the input method | the preedit of the input method (`TextInputEvent.Preedit`) is the composing range of the value and is underlined by the default `buildTextSpan`; the underline is cleared when the field loses the focus. A composing range that is not valid for the text panics with `TextEditingControllerComposingInvalid` in debug builds, like Flutter's assert | |
| `UndoHistory`, `UndoHistoryController`, `UndoManager` | `NewUndoHistory[T](value, onTriggered, focusNode, child, opts)` and `NewUndoHistoryController()`; the iOS `UndoManager` channel (`UndoManager.client`, `handlePlatformUndo`, `setUndoState`) is not ported. Undoing before the value was set panics with the tag `UndoHistoryValueNotSet` in debug builds | no platform has the iOS undo manager |
| `TapRegion`, `TapRegionSurface`, `TextFieldTapRegion`, `TapRegionRegistry` | `NewTapRegion(child, opts)` (`Disabled` is the negated `enabled`), `NewTapRegionSurface(child)` (a `WidgetsApp` has one) and `NewTextFieldTapRegion`; the callbacks take `*gestures.PointerDownEvent` and `*gestures.PointerUpEvent`; the surface does not take the semantics actions for a tap or a long press outside of a region. `TapRegionRegistryOf` without a surface panics with the tag `TapRegionRegistryMissing` in debug builds | the semantics actions are not wired to the surface yet |
| `TextSelectionGestureDetector` and `TextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilder` | the builder is a struct to embed (`InitTextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilder(b, self, delegate)`; `self` implements `TextSelectionGestureHandler`, the interface of the callbacks, so that the embedding builder overrides them); it takes the `TextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilderDelegate` of the field. Not ported: the force press, the magnifier, the stylus handwriting, the floating cursor and the spell check toolbar; the toolbar and the handles come with step 11.5. Triple taps select a line on Linux | the mobile features need mobile platforms |
| `ClipboardStatusNotifier` | `NewClipboardStatusNotifier(binding)` takes the binding (no `WidgetsBinding.instance`) and the clipboard check is a callback; its failure is reported with the tag `ClipboardStatusCheckFailed` and the status becomes unknown | no global binding |
| `CharacterBoundary`, `LineBoundary`, `ParagraphBoundary` and `DocumentBoundary` | they are in `services` since step 11.4 and implement `TextBoundary`, which has `GetTextBoundaryAt`, `GetLeadingTextBoundaryAt` and `GetTrailingTextBoundaryAt`; the results that Dart returns as `int?` are a second `bool`; offsets are UTF-8 bytes and the characters are grapheme clusters | the Go API conventions |
| `CompositedTransformTarget` and `CompositedTransformFollower` | `NewCompositedTransformTarget(link, child)` and `NewCompositedTransformFollower(link, child, opts)`; the options with a default of `true` are negated (`NoShowWhenUnlinked`) | the options struct convention |
| `ContextMenuController` and `ContextMenuButtonItem`; `SystemContextMenu` | `NewContextMenuController(opts)` keeps the shown menu in the `WidgetsBinding` of the context that shows it, not in static fields; Dart's static `removeAny` is `ContextMenuControllerRemoveAny(bc)`. `ContextMenuButtonItem.Equal` compares the type, the label and whether there is a callback (Go cannot compare functions). `MarkNeedsBuild` on a menu that is not shown panics with `ContextMenuNotShown`. `SystemContextMenu` (iOS) and the web context menu are not ported | no global state |
| `TextSelectionOverlay`, `SelectionOverlay`, `TextSelectionControls`, `TextSelectionToolbarAnchors`, `TextSelectionHandleControls` | `TextSelectionControls` is an interface of the handle builder, anchor and size; every controls value is Flutter's `TextSelectionHandleControls` (the context menu builder makes the toolbar), so the deprecated `buildToolbar`, `canCut`, `canCopy`, `canPaste`, `canSelectAll` and the `clipboardStatus` and `toolbarLocation` parameters are not ported. `EmptyTextSelectionControls` builds nothing. `TextSelectionOverlay.ShowToolbar` during the build or layout panics with `ShowToolbarDuringBuild`. The magnifier and the spell check suggestions toolbar are not ported. | the deprecated path and the mobile extras |
| `SelectableRegion`, `SelectionContainer`, `SelectionArea`'s basis, `MultiSelectableSelectionContainerDelegate`, `StaticSelectionContainerDelegate` | the delegates embed their Go base and are given `self` (`InitMultiSelectableSelectionContainerDelegate(&d.Base, d)`), so that the embedding type overrides `DispatchSelectionEventToChild`, `EnsureChildUpdated`, `CompareOrder` and the `Handle…` methods. `SelectionContainerDelegate` keeps the binding of its container, so that it still works while the container leaves the tree. `SelectableRegion.OnSelectionChanged` takes a `*rendering.SelectedContent` (nil for Dart's null). The tags are `SelectableRegionAlreadyFinalized`, `SelectableRegionMultipleSelectables`, `SelectableRegionUnknownSelectable`, `SelectableRegionCopyFailed` (the clipboard failed), `SelectableAlreadyAdded`, `SelectableNotInRegistrar`, `SelectionContainerDisabled` and `SelectionContainerNotLaidOut`. Not ported: the magnifier, the web context menu, the text processing actions and the share button. | Go has no virtual methods; the rest follows from the platforms Glutter has |
| `Text` and `RichText` in a selection container; `DefaultSelectionStyle` | a `Text` below a `SelectionContainer` wraps its `RichText` in a selection container of its own with a delegate that knows the paragraph (`_SelectableTextContainer`) and a `MouseRegion` with the cursor of `DefaultSelectionStyle` (the text cursor by default); the selection color is the option, then the style's, then `DefaultSelectionColor`. `DefaultSelectionStyleOf` returns `DefaultSelectionStyleFallback()` (no values) outside a style; `NewDefaultSelectionStyleMerge` keeps the parent's values for what is not set | the same behavior |
| `RenderParagraph` as a `Selectable` | `RenderParagraph` registers one selectable per fragment (the text between two `WidgetSpan`s) with its `SelectionRegistrar`; `Selections()` lists the selected ranges. The paragraph does word, paragraph (the text between line breaks) and direction extends of the selection with the same events as Flutter. The boundaries that cross a fragment, the origin boundary of a word at a placeholder and the baseline of a directional extend are not asserted (no input reaches those asserts). `PushHandleLayers` of a paragraph that is not attached with links panics with `SelectionHandlesOnDetached`; the tags `SelectionGranularityUnsupported`, `SelectionNotStarted` and `SelectionBaselineNaN` guard the events | the asserts that cannot fire are dropped |
| Lazy lists keep what is selected | every child of a `SliverChildBuilderDelegate`, `SliverChildListDelegate` and the two-dimensional delegates is wrapped in `AutomaticKeepAlive(_SelectionKeepAlive(child))`, as Flutter does: a child with a selection stays built while it scrolls out of view. `Scrollable` wraps its content in a selection container whose delegate scrolls the edge when a drag passes the boundary (Flutter's `_ScrollableSelectionHandler` and `_ScrollableSelectionContainerDelegate`) | the same behavior |
| `Form`, `FormField`, `FormState`, `FormFieldState` | `NewForm(child, opts)` and `NewFormField[T](builder, opts)`; the value and the error text are `foundation.Opt[T]` and `Opt[string]` (Dart's null); `Enabled` is the negated `Disabled`; `AutovalidateMode` has the zero value `AutovalidateModeUnspecified` that means disabled; `FormState.Fields()` lists the fields as `FormFieldStateBase`. `FormOf` without a form panics with `FormNotFound`. `OnWillPop` and `onPopInvoked` (deprecated) are not ported (`CanPop` and `OnPopInvokedWithResult` are); the restoration (`restorationId`) and the announcement of the first error to the semantics service are not ported | the options convention and the platforms Glutter has |
| `RawAutocomplete<T>` and its intents | `NewRawAutocomplete[T comparable](optionsViewBuilder, optionsBuilder, opts)`; the options builder gets a `deliver func([]T)` callback instead of returning a `FutureOr<Iterable<T>>` (a late answer is ignored when the text changed); `T` is `comparable`. `RawAutocompleteOnFieldSubmitted(key)` is `RawAutocomplete.onFieldSubmitted`. The tags are `RawAutocompleteNoField`, `RawAutocompleteFocusNodeMismatch`, `RawAutocompleteInitialValueAndController`, `RawAutocompleteKeyNotMounted` and `RawAutocompleteOptionsNotShowable`. The announcement of the number of results to the semantics service is not ported | no futures in the options builder |
| `RawMenuAnchor`, `RawMenuAnchorGroup`, `MenuController`, `DismissMenuAction` | `NewRawMenuAnchor(controller, overlayBuilder, child, opts)`; `MenuController.Open(position...)` takes an optional position; the options with a default of `true` are negated; `OnOpenRequested` gets an `Opt[ui.Offset]` and the callback that shows the overlay (Dart's `showOverlay`), `OnCloseRequested` the callback that hides it. `MenuControllerMaybeOf` and `MenuControllerMaybeIsOpenOf` are the `maybeOf` and `maybeIsOpenOf` statics. Using a controller that is not attached panics with `MenuControllerNotAttached` (Dart's `_anchor != null`), two anchors in one scope with `MenuControllerMultipleAnchors`; `MenuAnchorChildAlreadyAdded` and `MenuAnchorChildNotFound` guard the children of an anchor | the options convention |
| `RawTooltip`, `RawTooltipState.ensureTooltipVisible`, `RawTooltip.dismissAllToolTips` | `NewRawTooltip(semanticsTooltip, tooltipBuilder, child, opts)`; `semanticsTooltip` is `Opt[string]`; the options with a default of `true` are negated (`DisableTapToDismiss`, `DisableFeedback`); the default trigger mode is long press; `TooltipPositionDelegate` gets a `TooltipPositionContext` whose `PreferBelow()` is the negated `NoPreferBelow`. The open tooltips are kept in the `WidgetsBinding`, so `RawTooltipDismissAll(bc)` takes a context, and the `_ExclusiveMouseRegion` state is a value of the binding (`rendering.ExclusiveMouseRegionState`, `RenderExclusiveMouseRegion`). `WidgetsApp` has the `Focus` that dismisses the tooltips on Escape. A position delegate always relayouts the tooltip (Go cannot compare functions). The announcement of the tooltip to the semantics service is not ported | no global state |
| `Feedback` | `FeedbackForTap`, `FeedbackForLongPress` and the two `FeedbackWrapFor…` functions take the context; they send the tap and long press semantic events and play the click sound where the platform does; the haptic feedback is not ported | no haptic engine on the desktop |
| `gluttertest` `TestGesture.Down` and the pointers of the mouse | `TestGesture.Down(at)` presses the pointer again after `Up`. Every gesture gets a new pointer id, the mouse included (Flutter's `startGesture` does the same; before, all the mouse gestures shared one id, so a recognizer's entry of an old gesture panicked with `GestureArenaMemberUnknown` in the next one); the mouse device is the same | the arena of a pointer must not be reused |
| material-color-utilities is a Dart package: `MaterialDynamicColors` has static `DynamicColor` fields, each `DynamicScheme` variant is a subclass, `DynamicColor` caches the colors it made per scheme, `Hct` and the palettes are Dart classes with value equality and `hashCode`, and the package has image quantizers and `Score` | `material/internal/mcu` (step 12.1) ports it with ARGB as a `uint32`: the dynamic colors are package variables that `init` sets (they refer to each other, which Go's initialization order does not allow in a variable declaration; they are never written afterwards), the cache of the colors is in the `DynamicScheme` (a map by `*DynamicColor`, so there is no shared mutable state, and a scheme is not safe for concurrent use), a variant is a constructor (`NewSchemeTonalSpot(hct, isDark, contrastLevel)`) that returns a `*DynamicScheme` with its `Variant`, `Hct` and `TonalPalette` have `Equal` instead of `==` and `hashCode`, `Hct` has `SetHue`, `SetChroma` and `SetTone`, `TemperatureCache` keys its temperatures by ARGB (the equality of `Hct`) and sorts stably, `ViewingConditionsOpts` uses `foundation.Opt` for the parameters whose default is not the zero value, and `quantize` and `score` are not ported, because they serve `ColorScheme.fromImageProvider`, which Glutter does not have (the CPU decoding of an image and the quantizing of its pixels can come with it) | Go has no static initialization order for cyclic values, and no shared mutable state (§12); the rest is the Go form of the same code. The exact-color tests of the Dart package pass (HCT round trip of all 16.7 million colors, CAM16, palettes, temperature, blend, the dynamic color values and the scheme correctness constraints) |
| Dart's `WidgetState` / `MaterialState` model: `WidgetStateProperty<T>`, `WidgetStateColor`, `WidgetStateBorderSide`, `WidgetStateOutlinedBorder`, `WidgetStateTextStyle`, `WidgetStateMouseCursor`, `WidgetStateMapper`, `WidgetStatePropertyAll`, the `WidgetStateProperty.lerp`, the `MaterialState` aliases and the `MaterialStateMixin` | `widgets.WidgetStates` is a bitset (`NewWidgetStates(...)`) and `WidgetStateProperty[T]` an interface with `Resolve(WidgetStates) T`. A `Color`, a `BorderSide`, an `OutlinedBorder` and a `TextStyle` are plain values in Go (a `ui.Color` cannot also be a property, as Dart's `WidgetStateColor extends Color`), so `WidgetStateColorResolveWith`, `WidgetStateColorFromMap`... are functions that make a `WidgetStateProperty[T]` of that value type; a property that may be null is a `WidgetStateProperty[Opt[T]]` for `BorderSide`. `WidgetStateResolveAs[T](value any, states)` resolves a value that is either a `T` or a property of `T`. `WidgetStateMouseCursor` is both a `services.MouseCursor` and a property, as in Dart. `WidgetStateMap[T]` is an ordered slice of entries (Dart's map order matters: the first match wins). `WidgetStatesConstraint` is an interface with `And`, `Or`, `Not` functions instead of operators. The property implementations are pointer types, because the struct of a function cannot be compared. The `MaterialState*` deprecated aliases and the mixin are not ported | Go has no operator overloading, no implicit conversion from a value to a property, and cannot compare functions |
| `ColorScheme` | the fields are private and read through the getters (`Primary()`, `PrimaryContainer()`...), which have Flutter's fallbacks (an unset container color is the main color, an unset surface color is `surface`...); the optional colors are `foundation.Opt[ui.Color]`; `ColorSchemeCopy` and `CopyWith` are written by hand (they materialize the resolved colors, as Flutter's `copyWith` reads the getters); `ColorScheme.light()`, `dark()`, `highContrastLight()` and `highContrastDark()` are `NewColorSchemeLight`... with `ColorSchemeVariantOpts`; `ColorScheme.fromSeed` is `ColorSchemeFromSeed(seed, ColorSchemeFromSeedOpts{Brightness, DynamicSchemeVariant, ContrastLevel, Overrides})` (the `ColorSchemeVariantOpts` field replaces the colors that the dynamic scheme makes); `ColorScheme.of(context)` is `ColorSchemeOf(bc)`; `ColorScheme.fromImageProvider` is not ported | Go has no `const` constructors with defaults; the image quantizer and scorer of material-color-utilities are not ported (12.1) |
| `Colors`, `MaterialColor`, `MaterialAccentColor`, `ColorSwatch` | `Colors` is a package variable of the struct type `ColorsType` (its fields are the colors and the swatches: `Colors.Blue`, `Colors.Black54`); a swatch is a `MaterialColor` that wraps a `*painting.ColorSwatch[int]`, whose main color is `Colors.Blue.Primary` (Dart's `MaterialColor extends Color`), and `Colors.blue[500]` is `Colors.Blue.Shade500()` or `Colors.Blue.At(500)`; `Colors.primaries` and `Colors.accents` are the fields `Colors.Primaries` and `Colors.Accents` | `ui.Color` is a struct and cannot be extended |
| `TextTheme`, `Typography`, `ScriptCategory` | `TextTheme` has exported `*painting.TextStyle` fields (a nil style is Dart's null) and is made with `NewTextTheme(TextThemeOpts{...})`; `CopyWith`, `Merge`, `Apply(TextThemeApply{...})` and `TextThemeLerp` follow Flutter's; `TextThemeOf(bc)` and `TextThemePrimaryOf(bc)` are `TextTheme.of` and `primaryOf`. The constant themes of `Typography` are package variables (`TypographyBlackMountainView`, `TypographyEnglishLike2018`, `TypographyEnglishLike2021`...), generated once from `typography.dart`; the fonts are those of Flutter (`Roboto` and the Helsinki fallbacks on Linux; the Roboto bundled with Glutter is the first). `Typography.material2018(platform: p)` is `NewTypographyMaterial2018(TypographyOpts{Platform: Some(p)})`; the platform defaults to Android as in Dart; `NewTypography` is `Typography.new` (which is `material2018`). `Typography.material2021` takes the color scheme in `Typography2021Opts` | the options struct convention; Go cannot make a `const` text theme |
| `ThemeData` | `NewThemeData(ThemeDataOpts{...})` derives every field that is not set exactly as Flutter's factory does (Material 3 from the color scheme, Material 2 from the primary swatch), and returns a `*ThemeData` with exported fields (`theme.PrimaryColor`, `theme.TextTheme`); `Brightness()` is the one of the color scheme. `ThemeData.from`, `light`, `dark` and `fallback` are `NewThemeDataFrom`, `NewThemeDataLight`, `NewThemeDataDark` and `NewThemeDataFallback` (the latter three take an optional `useMaterial3` bool). `CopyWith(ThemeDataCopy{...})` is written by hand because Flutter's takes a `brightness` that replaces only the brightness of the color scheme (`ThemeDataCopy.Brightness`). `ThemeData.extension<T>()` is `ThemeDataExtension[T](theme)`; a `ThemeExtension` is an interface with `CopyWith() ThemeExtension` and `Lerp(other, t)` (an extension is found by its dynamic type, which is what Dart's `T` is). `ThemeData.localize` is `ThemeDataLocalize` and is not memoized (Flutter caches the last five results by identity in a static; Glutter has no global mutable state), and `ThemeData.estimateBrightnessForColor` is `EstimateBrightnessForColor`. `pageTransitionsTheme` is in `ThemeData` since step 12.4 (`ThemeData.PageTransitionsTheme`, a `*PageTransitionsTheme`; the lerp picks the nearer end, as Flutter's). `ThemeData` has `AppBarTheme`, `BannerTheme`, `BottomAppBarTheme` and `SnackBarTheme` since step 13.1 (each a data struct, so a `ThemeData` with none set has the zero value, which means "not set"). `ThemeData` has `InputDecorationTheme`, `TextSelectionTheme` and `DropdownMenuTheme` since step 13.5 (`InputDecorationThemeData` is not a plain struct of options: its `Merge` and the lerp, which picks the nearer end, are Flutter's). Not yet in `ThemeData`: the other component themes, `adaptations`, `cupertinoOverrideTheme`, `scrollbarTheme`, `splashFactory` and `useSystemColors`; the step that ports each adds its field to `ThemeData`, `ThemeDataOpts`, `ThemeDataCopy`, `ThemeDataLerp`, `Equal` and `DebugFillProperties` | the options struct convention; no global mutable state; the pieces do not exist yet |
| `VisualDensity`, `MaterialTapTargetSize`, `kThemeAnimationDuration`, `constants.dart` | `VisualDensity` is a comparable value struct made with `NewVisualDensity(VisualDensityOpts{...})`; its constants are the functions `VisualDensityStandard()`, `VisualDensityComfortable()` and `VisualDensityCompact()`; `MaterialTapTargetSize` has the zero value `MaterialTapTargetSizeUnspecified`, the platform's default. The `k` prefix of the constants of `constants.dart` is dropped (`ToolbarHeight`, `MinInteractiveDimension`, `ThemeAnimationDuration`); `kDefaultIconLightColor`/`kDefaultIconDarkColor` and the two `EdgeInsets` are package variables | Go has no `const` for structs; names follow SPEC §12.1 |
| `Theme`, `AnimatedTheme`, `ThemeDataTween` | `NewTheme(data, child)`, `ThemeOf(bc)`, `ThemeBrightnessOf(bc)`, `ThemeMaybeBrightnessOf(bc)`, `NewAnimatedTheme(data, child, AnimatedThemeOpts{...})`, `NewThemeDataTween`. `Theme` provides the `IconTheme` of its data but not a `CupertinoTheme` (there is none) but it does provide the `DefaultSelectionStyle` from `textSelectionTheme` (step 13.5); `ThemeOf` takes the script category of the text geometry from `MaterialLocalizations`, `englishLike` without them. The reference example of §12.12 and `Icons.add` calls get `()` |

The debug assertion of `MaterialApp.router` is the tag `MaterialAppRouterMissing`; those of the `WidgetsApp` come at build, as in Flutter. `scaffoldMessengerKey` is `ScaffoldMessengerKey` (a `widgets.AnyGlobalKey`, such as `NewGlobalKey[*ScaffoldMessengerState]()`) and the app has a `ScaffoldMessenger` (step 13.1). | `MaterialScrollBehavior` | `NewMaterialScrollBehavior()`: the platform is the one of the theme; a vertical scrollable of a desktop platform gets the `RawScrollbar` of the widgets layer until the Material `Scrollbar` is ported (step 13.9); there are no overscroll indicators (`GlowingOverscrollIndicator` and `StretchingOverscrollIndicator` are Android only) | the Material `Scrollbar` is in a later step |
| `MaterialPageRoute`, `MaterialPage`, `MaterialRouteTransitionMixin` | `NewMaterialPageRoute(builder, MaterialPageRouteOpts{PageRouteOpts, MaintainState})` (`MaintainState` is an `Opt[bool]`, true by default), `NewMaterialPage(child, MaterialPageOpts{PageOpts, MaintainState, FullscreenDialog, DisallowSnapshotting})`; the mixin is `MaterialRouteBase` (embed it, call `InitMaterialRouteBase(&b, self, opts)`) over a `MaterialRoute` interface that has `BuildContent`. The route of a page reads the page from its settings, as Flutter's does. Dart's fallback duration of 300 microseconds in `transitionDuration` is dropped: the theme always has a builder (it falls back to the zoom transition) | the mixin becomes a base struct (§12.5) |
| `PageTransitionsTheme`, `PageTransitionsBuilder` (`widgets/page_transitions_builder.dart`), `ZoomPageTransitionsBuilder`, `FadeForwardsPageTransitionsBuilder`, `FadeUpwardsPageTransitionsBuilder`, `OpenUpwardsPageTransitionsBuilder`, `DualTransitionBuilder` | `NewPageTransitionsTheme(PageTransitionsThemeOpts{Builders})`; the builders are comparable value structs (`NewZoomPageTransitionsBuilder(opts)`) that embed `widgets.PageTransitionsBuilderBase` (300 ms and no delegated transition; a builder with another duration overrides both `TransitionDuration` and `ReverseTransitionDuration`, as the base cannot see the type that embeds it). The geometry is a `foundation.ValueListenable[ScaffoldGeometry]`, with `Opt` fields. Flutter's `_BodyBoxConstraints` (the body builder reads the heights of the bottom widgets and the app bar from the constraints) is `LayoutBuilderOpts.Extra` (below). A floating snack bar that does not fit panics with `FloatingSnackBarOffScreen`. | `IconButton` (`.filled`, `.filledTonal`, `.outlined`), `IconButtonTheme`, `IconButtonThemeData` | `NewIconButton(onPressed, icon, IconButtonOpts)`, `NewIconButtonFilled`, `NewIconButtonFilledTonal`, `NewIconButtonOutlined`; the options are those of Flutter (`IconSize`, `VisualDensity`, `SplashRadius`, `Color`, `DisabledColor`, `Constraints` and `IsSelected` are `Opt`s; `IsSelected` none is a button that does not toggle, and the toggle `selected` state follows it); `IconButtonStyleFrom(IconButtonStyleFromOpts)`. A Material 3 button is a `ButtonStyleButton` (`iconButtonM3`) whose defaults depend on the variant and on whether it toggles; the button of Material 2 is an `InkResponse`. **Deviation:** Dart's `identical(iconTheme.color, kDefaultIconDarkColor)` (the icon theme still has the default color) compares the colors by value. The tag `IconButtonSplashRadius` is the assertion of the splash radius. `BackButton`, `CloseButton`, `DrawerButton` and `EndDrawerButton` build an `IconButton` and take the icon of the `ActionIconTheme` (`ActionIconThemeData` with the four builders, `NewActionIconTheme`, `ActionIconThemeOf(bc)`; the field of `ThemeData` is the data itself, whose builders are nil for Dart's `null`, not a nullable one); `ActionButtonOpts` has `Style` | the options struct convention; Go has no `identical` for a value |
| `FloatingActionButton` (`.small`, `.large`, `.extended`), `FloatingActionButtonThemeData`, `FloatingActionButtonTheme`, `RawMaterialButton` | `NewFloatingActionButton(onPressed, child, FloatingActionButtonOpts)` (the `mini` argument is `Mini`), `NewFloatingActionButtonSmall`, `NewFloatingActionButtonLarge`, `NewFloatingActionButtonExtended(onPressed, label, FloatingActionButtonExtendedOpts{FloatingActionButtonOpts, Icon, NoExtended, ExtendedIconLabelSpacing, ExtendedPadding, ExtendedTextStyle})` (`NoExtended` is `isExtended: false`; `IsExtended()` reads it). `HeroTag` is an `any` whose default is a tag that all the buttons share, and `NoHero` is `heroTag: null`. The defaults are `fabDefaults(bc, type, hasChild)`, with the Material 2 and Material 3 values of Flutter. `RawMaterialButton` has the options of Flutter with `Opt`s for the elevations, the density, the constraints and the duration, and `NoEnableFeedback`; **deviation:** the text color and the shape are not resolved from the states (a `WidgetStateColor` text color and a `WidgetStateOutlinedBorder` shape are not used by the floating action button). The tags `FloatingActionButtonElevationNegative` and `RawMaterialButtonElevationNegative` | the options struct convention |
| `FloatingActionButtonLocation`, `StandardFabLocation`, `FabTopOffsetY`..., `FloatingActionButtonAnimator`, `kFloatingActionButtonMargin`, `kFloatingActionButtonSegue`, `kFloatingActionButtonTurnInterval`, `kMiniButtonOffsetAdjustment` | `FloatingActionButtonLocation` is an interface (`GetOffset(ScaffoldPrelayoutGeometry)`) and each static of Flutter is a package level value: `FloatingActionButtonLocationStartTop`, `…MiniStartTop`, `…CenterTop`, `…EndTop`, `…StartFloat`, `…EndFloat`, `…StartDocked`, `…EndDocked`, `…EndContained` and the others of the table, which are `*fabLocation` with a `String()` of `FloatingActionButtonLocation.endFloat`. `StandardFabLocation` is an interface (`GetOffsetX`, `GetOffsetY`, `IsMini`) with `StandardFabLocationOffset(l, geometry)`, and the mixins of Flutter (`FabTopOffsetY`, `FabFloatOffsetY`, `FabDockedOffsetY`, `FabContainedOffsetY`, `FabStartOffsetX`, `FabCenterOffsetX`, `FabEndOffsetX`) are functions that an app composes. `FloatingActionButtonAnimator` is an interface (`GetOffset`, `GetScaleAnimation`, `GetRotationAnimation`, `GetAnimationRestart`) with the values `FloatingActionButtonAnimatorScaling` and `FloatingActionButtonAnimatorNoAnimation`; the constants are `FloatingActionButtonMargin`, `FloatingActionButtonSegue`, `FloatingActionButtonTurnInterval` and `MiniButtonOffsetAdjustment`. Flutter's `switch` over the top locations, which keeps a snack bar from sitting above the button, is `fabLocationIsTop` | mixins and statics become functions and values |
| `SegmentedButton<T>`, `ButtonSegment<T>`, `SegmentedButtonState<T>`, `SegmentedButtonThemeData`, `SegmentedButtonTheme` | `NewSegmentedButton[T comparable](segments, selected []T, onSelectionChanged func([]T), SegmentedButtonOpts)`: the selection is an ordered slice (Dart's insertion ordered `Set`), `onSelectionChanged` is positional and nil disables the button (a required nullable argument; in Flutter it is optional and named), the negated booleans are `NoShowSelectedIcon` (`showSelectedIcon`), and `ButtonSegment.Disabled` is `enabled: false`; the segments are identified by their value (the `statesControllers` of the state are keyed by it, not by the segment object). `SegmentedButtonStyleFrom(SegmentedButtonStyleFromOpts{ButtonStyleFromOpts, SelectedForegroundColor, SelectedBackgroundColor})`. The tags `SegmentedButtonNoSegments`, `SegmentedButtonNoSelection`, `SegmentedButtonMultipleSelected` and `ButtonSegmentEmpty` are the assertions of the constructor. The layout is `_RenderSegmentedButton` (`renderSegmentedButton`) | Go generics; the options struct convention |
| `Drawer`, `DrawerController`, `DrawerControllerState`, `DrawerHeader`, `DrawerThemeData`, `DrawerTheme`, `DrawerAlignment` | `NewDrawer(DrawerOpts{...})`, `NewDrawerController(child, alignment, DrawerControllerOpts{...})` (`DrawerControllerState` is exported, with `Open()` and `Close()`; `DrawerControllerOf(bc)`, tag `DrawerControllerMissing`, and `MaybeOf`), `NewDrawerHeader(DrawerHeaderOpts{...})` (`Margin: painting.EdgeInsetsZero` is Flutter's `margin: null`). The `ListTileTheme` that Flutter puts around the drawer comes with `ListTile` (step 13.9) | the options struct convention |

The keys (arrows, Home, End, Escape, Enter) are Flutter's shortcuts and actions; the focus moves to the first item with Down and to the last with Up. `Animated` (Flutter 3.35) runs the height and opacity animation of the menu and `OnAnimationStatusChanged` reports it. **Deviation:** Flutter avoids the display features (the hinge of a foldable) with `DisplayFeatureSubScreen`; there are none on the desktop, so the whole overlay is the allowed area | the options struct convention |

Popup menu routes have `PoppedThen` (below) | futures are channels (§12.1) |
| `DropdownButton<T>`, `DropdownMenuItem<T>`, `DropdownButtonHideUnderline`, `DropdownButtonBuilder` | `NewDropdownButton[T comparable](items, onChanged func(Opt[T]), DropdownButtonOpts[T]{...})` (nil `onChanged` disables it; tags `DropdownButtonValueNotUnique`, `DropdownButtonItemHeight`, `DropdownButtonSelectedItemBuilder` and `DropdownButtonRouteOpen`), `NewDropdownMenuItem[T](child, opts)` (`Disabled` is `enabled: false`), `NewDropdownButtonHideUnderline(child)` and `DropdownButtonHideUnderlineAt(bc)`. A field that is already a `foundation.Opt` takes the replacement as it is when it is set (the generator took the value inside the `Opt` before 13.1, which did not compile) | the four classes have a `copyWith` that is not a plain field-by-field replacement |

The decoration animates the label, the border, the helper and the error as Flutter does (the numbers of the label match `input_decorator_test.dart`) | the options struct convention; null in a state property |
| `InputBorder`, `UnderlineInputBorder`, `OutlineInputBorder`, `InputBorder.none` | `InputBorder` is an interface (`BorderSide()`, `IsOutline()`, `CopyWithBorderSide`, `PaintGap`) that is also a `painting.ShapeBorder`; `InputBorderNone`, `NewUnderlineInputBorder(opts)`, `NewOutlineInputBorder(opts)` (`GapPadding` and the corners are checked: tags `OutlineInputBorderGapPadding`, `OutlineInputBorderCorners`, `InputBorderGapPercentage`). The gap in the top edge is drawn with the path of `_gapBorderPath`. **Not ported:** `InputBorder` as a base class of other borders (an app implements the interface) | the options struct convention |

They are needed by `_RenderDecoration` and are public for apps | Go has no mixins |

The tags `TextFieldObscuringCharacterInvalid`, `TextFieldMaxLinesInvalid`, `TextFieldMinLinesInvalid`, `TextFieldMinLinesGreaterThanMaxLines`, `TextFieldExpandsWithLines`, `TextFieldObscuredMultiline`, `TextFieldMaxLengthInvalid`, `TextFieldNewlineActionWithTextKeyboard` and `TextFieldStyleNotInheriting` are the assertions. `NewTextFormField(TextFormFieldOpts{TextFieldOpts, InitialValue, ForceErrorText, OnFieldSubmitted, OnSaved, Validator, ErrorBuilder, AutovalidateMode})` takes every parameter of `TextField` (tags `TextFormFieldInitialValueWithController`, `TextFormFieldErrorBuilderWithErrorText`). **Deviation:** Dart's `TextFormField` is a `FormField<String>` with its own `FormFieldState` subclass; here it is a stateful widget that builds a `FormField[string]` and keeps the controller in sync through two hooks: `FormFieldOpts.OnReset` and the new `FormFieldOpts.OnDidChange`, which stands for the `didChange` override. **Not ported:** the restoration of the state (`restorationId`), the spell check, the magnifier (`magnifierConfiguration`), the stylus handwriting (`stylusHandwritingEnabled`, `scribbleEnabled`), `contentInsertionConfiguration`, the deprecated `toolbarOptions` and the system context menu (`SystemContextMenu`); autofill groups (the editable text is the autofill client). Tags `DropdownMenuFilterCallbackWithoutFilter`, `DropdownMenuTrailingIconFocusNodeWithoutIcon`, `DropdownMenuDecorationBuilderWithTexts`. `inputDecorationTheme` is the data (`*InputDecorationThemeData`), not the widget. | `DropdownButtonFormField<T>` | `NewDropdownButtonFormField[T comparable](items, onChanged, DropdownButtonFormFieldOpts[T]{...})`; `isDense` is true by default (`NotDense`), the item height is none by default; `initialValue` replaces the deprecated `value`. Tags `DropdownButtonFormFieldValueNotUnique`, `DropdownButtonFormFieldErrorBuilderWithErrorText`. **Deviation:** a stateful widget that builds a `FormField[T]`, as `TextFormField` (see above) | see `TextFormField` |
| `TextSelectionThemeData`, `TextSelectionTheme`, `MaterialTextSelectionControls`, `DesktopTextSelectionControls`, `AdaptiveTextSelectionToolbar`, `DesktopTextSelectionToolbar`, `DesktopTextSelectionToolbarButton`, `SelectionArea` | `NewTextSelectionTheme(data, child)` (the child is wrapped in a `DefaultSelectionStyle`; the `Theme` and the `MaterialApp` read the data), `TextSelectionThemeOf(bc)`, `MaterialTextSelectionHandleControls` and `DesktopTextSelectionHandleControls` (read-only values; `MaterialTextSelectionControls` and `DesktopTextSelectionControls` are the same types, since the context menu is not part of the controls), `NewAdaptiveTextSelectionToolbar…` (one constructor for each of Flutter's named ones; `AdaptiveTextSelectionToolbarGetButtonLabel` and `…GetAdaptiveButtons` are the statics), `NewDesktopTextSelectionToolbar(anchor, children)` (tag `DesktopTextSelectionToolbarNoChildren`), `NewDesktopTextSelectionToolbarButton(onPressed, child)` and `…ButtonText(bc, onPressed, text)`, `widgets.DesktopTextSelectionToolbarLayoutDelegate`, `NewSelectionArea(child, opts)` with `SelectionAreaState.SelectableRegion()`. **Deviation:** the toolbar of every platform is the desktop one: the Material `TextSelectionToolbar` of Android (with its overflow menu) and the Cupertino toolbars are not ported; the deprecated `buildToolbar`, `canCut`, `canCopy`, `canPaste`, `canSelectAll` and `handleX` of the controls are not ported either (`TextSelectionControls` has `BuildHandle`, `GetHandleAnchor` and `GetHandleSize`). **Not ported:** the magnifier of the handles (`TextMagnifier`) and the spell check suggestions toolbar | no Cupertino library; the features are not in the framework |
| `gluttertest` `Tester.Center` and `Tester.BottomRight` | they use `LocalToGlobal`, so a widget under a transform (the floating label of a text field) has its position on the screen, as Flutter's `getCenter`/`getBottomRight` do | the first versions ignored the paint transforms |
| `EditableTextState.renderEditable` after the field left the tree | `RenderEditable()` is nil once the element of the editable is not active, so the callbacks of the composition and the post-frame callbacks that come after a focused field was removed do nothing, instead of reporting `FindRenderObjectInactive` | Flutter's `renderEditable` has the same guard through `mounted` |

### 12.11 One import for apps

Flutter's `material.dart` re-exports `widgets.dart`, which exports all of `animation.dart` and `painting.dart` and the names that its libraries re-export from `foundation`, `rendering`, `gestures`, `services`, `physics`, `scheduler` and `dart:ui`. Glutter gets the same effect with `cmd/glutter-gen reexport` (run it in the root of the module; `go generate ./material` does), which type-checks those packages and writes `material/widgets_reexport_gen.go`:
- type aliases (generic aliases included)
- thin wrapper functions (generic ones too), which the compiler inlines
- the constants and the variables, copied (a read-only value such as `Curves` or `Colors` is a copy; a variable of a basic type is not copied, as a copy would not follow the writes to the original, and the file says which ones)

The packages are re-exported whole (`widgets`, `animation`, `painting`) or by the names that Flutter exports (`DefaultSpecs` in `cmd/glutter-gen/internal/reexport`): a Go identifier belongs to a name when it is that name, or starts with it (optionally after `New`) and goes on with a capital letter or a digit, so that the constructors, the options and the functions of a class come with it. `foundation.Opt`, `Some` and `None` are re-exported too, as the options structs need them. When two packages have an identifier of the same name the first one wins (`widgets`, `animation`, `painting`, then the others), and an identifier that package `material` declares itself is not re-exported (the file lists them). A function whose signature has a type that another package cannot name (an unexported type, or a type of an `internal` package) is left out.

Apps import only `m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"`. The widgets layer itself never imports `material`.

Apps import only `m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"`. The widgets layer itself never imports `material`.

### 12.12 Reference example

This is `examples/counter/main.go` without its header comment and without the code of `GLUTTER_DEV_TAPS` (`InitState` and `devTaps`, which start the counter at that number in debug builds, for the proof screenshot). It differs from the first draft of this section in the names that Glutter's API actually has: `m.Colors.Blue.Primary`, `m.Icons.Add()` (§12.10) and the `TextTheme.HeadlineMedium` field. It also sets the window and turns the debug banner off, so the screenshot shows the page only.

```go
package main

import (
	"log"
	"strconv"

	m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"
)

func main() {
	err := m.RunApp(NewMyApp(), m.RunAppOpts{
		AppID:  "dev.glutter.counter",
		Window: m.WindowConfig{Title: "Counter", Size: m.Size{Width: 480, Height: 640}},
	})
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatal(err)
	}
}

type MyApp struct{ m.StatelessWidget }

func NewMyApp() *MyApp { return &MyApp{} }

func (a *MyApp) Build(bc m.BuildContext) m.Widget {
	return m.NewMaterialApp(m.MaterialAppOpts{
		Title:                      "Counter",
		Theme:                      m.NewThemeData(m.ThemeDataOpts{ColorSchemeSeed: m.Some(m.Colors.Blue.Primary)}),
		DebugShowCheckedModeBanner: m.Some(false),
		Home:                       NewCounterPage("Counter"),
	})
}

type CounterPage struct {
	m.StatefulWidget
	title string
}

func NewCounterPage(title string, opts ...m.WidgetOpts) *CounterPage {
	return &CounterPage{StatefulWidget: m.NewStatefulWidgetBase(opts...), title: title}
}

func (p *CounterPage) Title() string        { return p.title }
func (p *CounterPage) CreateState() m.State { return &counterPageState{} }

type counterPageState struct {
	m.StateOf[*CounterPage]
	count int
}

func (s *counterPageState) increment() { s.SetState(func() { s.count++ }) }

func (s *counterPageState) Build(bc m.BuildContext) m.Widget {
	return m.NewScaffold(m.ScaffoldOpts{
		AppBar: m.NewAppBar(m.AppBarOpts{Title: m.NewText(s.Widget().Title())}),
		Body: m.NewCenter(
			m.NewColumn([]m.Widget{
				m.NewText("You have pushed the button this many times:"),
				m.NewText(strconv.Itoa(s.count), m.TextOpts{Style: m.ThemeOf(bc).TextTheme.HeadlineMedium}),
			}, m.ColumnOpts{MainAxisAlignment: m.MainAxisAlignmentCenter}),
		),
		FloatingActionButton: m.NewFloatingActionButton(s.increment, m.NewIcon(m.Icons.Add()),
			m.FloatingActionButtonOpts{Tooltip: "Increment"}),
	})
}
```

---

## 13. Threading and lifecycle

- `m.RunApp(app Widget, opts ...RunAppOpts) error` must run on the main goroutine (§8.4); `RunWidget` is the same without the default `View`. It returns when the last window closes or after `SystemNavigatorPop`. Environment failures come back as tagged errors (§14.1).
- `RunAppOpts` include:
  - `Assets fs.FS` for the `AssetBundle`
  - `AppID`, `Window` (the first window's config)
  - `Backend` (overrides `GLUTTER_BACKEND`)
  - `ErrorReporter` and `ErrorWidgetBuilder` (§14.2)
  - `Logger *slog.Logger`
  - `Debug *DebugOptions` (§14.3)
- The UI thread owns the element tree, render tree, `ChangeNotifier`s, controllers and GL. From other goroutines, only these are safe:
  - `State.SetState`
  - `WidgetsBindingOf(bc).Post(fn)`
  - the `desktop` service calls (they take a `context.Context` and deliver results with `Post`)
- `debug` builds detect calls made from the wrong goroutine on the main framework entry points and report them with tag `WrongThread`.
- App lifecycle maps to `AppLifecycleState`:
  - `Resumed`: focused
  - `Inactive`: visible but unfocused
  - `Hidden`: minimized or all windows hidden
  - `Detached`: shutting down
  - `Paused` is not used on desktop, as in Flutter's desktop embedders.

## 14. Errors and diagnostics

### 14.1 Environment errors

These are returned from `RunApp`, image/font loading and `desktop` calls, always with tags from `rm4n0s/errors`. The initial tags are:
- `LibraryLoadFailed`, `NoDisplay`, `BackendUnknown`, `BackendUnavailable`, `WaylandConnectFailed`, `WaylandProtocolMissing`, `WaylandProtocolError`, `X11OpenDisplayFailed`, `IdleInhibitUnavailable`
- `EGLLoadFailed`, `EGLInitFailed`, `EGLNoConfig`, `GLES3Unsupported`, `ShaderCompileFailed`
- `AssetNotFound`, `ImageDecodeFailed`, `FontLoadFailed`
- `DBusConnectFailed`, `TrayUnavailable`, `NotificationsUnavailable`, `FileChooserUnavailable`, `OpenURIUnavailable`
- `MultipleOptions`, `WrongThread`

Every tag has a route test.

### 14.2 Programmer errors and panics in app code (D15)

Glutter follows Flutter. Flutter reports failed asserts and exceptions through `FlutterError.onError`, replaces the failing subtree with `ErrorWidget`, and keeps the app running.

- **Signaling**: `Build`, layout, paint and callbacks never return errors. Bugs panic: Go runtime panics, and failed debug checks, which panic with a tagged `*errors.Error` (e.g. `SetStateAfterDispose`).
- **Recovery is done by the library.** App code never writes `recover()`. The framework recovers at the same points where Flutter catches exceptions:

  | Recovery point | Flutter equivalent |
  |---|---|
  | `Build` of every element | `ComponentElement.performRebuild` |
  | layout and paint of every render object | `RenderObject._reportException` |
  | gesture callbacks | `GestureRecognizer.invokeCallback` |
  | `Listenable` and animation listeners | `ChangeNotifier.notifyListeners` |
  | scheduler frame/post-frame callbacks and timers | `SchedulerBinding` |
  | image and font stream listeners | `ImageStreamCompleter` |

- **Reporting**: each recovered panic becomes a tagged error (`BuildPanicked`, `LayoutPanicked`, `PaintPanicked`, `CallbackPanicked`) carrying the panic value, the stack and the element path. A panic that is already a tagged `*errors.Error` (a failed debug check) is reported with its own tag. It goes to `RunAppOpts.ErrorReporter func(error)`, which the binding passes to every part of the framework (§12.10). A part that has no reporter does not recover. The default reporter logs `appErr.ToJson()` through the app's `slog.Logger`. An app can forward errors to its own service, or call `os.Exit` to crash instead.
- **Display**: the failing subtree is replaced by `RunAppOpts.ErrorWidgetBuilder func(error) Widget`. The default is Flutter's: a red box with the message in debug builds, a plain gray box in release builds.
- **Goroutines**: Go can't catch a panic in a goroutine started with a bare `go` statement, so it crashes the program. Use `State.Go(func(ctx context.Context))`, which runs the function with the state's `Lifetime()` context under the same recovery and reporting.
- **Tests**: `gluttertest` fails a test on any reported error unless the test takes it with `tester.TakeError()`, Flutter's `takeException()`.
- **Constructors** never return errors or error widgets (D1).

### 14.3 Debugging aids

- Flags in `rendering`/`widgets`: `DebugPaintSizeEnabled`, `DebugPaintBaselinesEnabled`, `DebugPaintLayerBordersEnabled`, `DebugRepaintRainbowEnabled`, `DebugPrintRebuildDirtyWidgets`, `DebugPrintLayouts`, `DebugProfileBuildsEnabled`. These are fields of a `DebugOptions` value passed through `RunAppOpts`, not global variables.
- `DebugDumpApp`, `DebugDumpRenderTree`, `DebugDumpLayerTree`, `DebugDumpSemanticsTree`, `DebugDumpFocusTree`.
- A performance overlay (frame build and raster time graphs).
- `runtime/trace` regions around the frame phases.

---

## 15. Performance targets

These are measured on a 2020 laptop with an Intel Iris Xe or similar GPU and Mesa, at 1920×1080 and a device pixel ratio of 1.25.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Idle CPU (no animation, no input) | ~0%: no frames scheduled, no timers armed |
| Frame rate (gallery screen, ~500 visible widgets, one animation) | the display's refresh rate with no dropped frames |
| UI thread CPU per frame, animation-only frame (no rebuild) | < 2 ms |
| UI thread CPU per frame, full rebuild of the gallery screen | < 8 ms |
| Steady-state allocations, animation-only frame | ≤ 1 KB per frame |
| Cold start to first frame | < 150 ms (fonts cached) |
| Resident memory, counter example | < 60 MB |

Benchmarks (`go test -bench`) in `rendering`, `widgets` and `internal/gpu/soft` track these numbers. Regressions above 10% need a justification.

## 16. Build and runtime requirements

**Building an app**: Go 1.27+ and nothing else. `CGO_ENABLED=0 go build` produces a binary that loads the system libraries at runtime. It's dynamically linked against glibc's loader, which is how purego reaches `dlopen`. Cross-compiling for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 from any OS works.

**Contributing**: Go 1.27+ plus a C compiler, **only** because `go test -race` requires cgo. When cgo is enabled, purego uses it transparently, so the same code is tested both ways (§17). No `-devel` packages, `wayland-scanner` or `wayland-protocols` are needed.

**Runtime**:
- **Required**:
  - a Wayland compositor with `xdg_wm_base`, or an X server
  - an EGL driver with OpenGL ES 3.0 (Mesa ≥ 20, NVIDIA through glvnd, or llvmpipe)
  - the runtime libraries in §8.1. They're installed on every Linux desktop (Wayland compositors and Xwayland depend on them), and a missing one is reported as a tagged error.
- **Optional**:
  - a D-Bus session bus, which every desktop session has; all the services below use it
  - xdg-desktop-portal plus a backend, for file dialogs, settings and open URI
  - a StatusNotifierItem host, for the tray (KDE, XFCE, Cinnamon, and GNOME with the AppIndicator extension)
  - a notification daemon
  - at-spi2-core, for accessibility

## 17. Testing strategy

- **Unit tests** are table-driven and sit next to the code (CLAUDE.md).
- **Widget tests** (`gluttertest`):
  - `t := gluttertest.New(tb)`, then `PumpWidget`, `Pump(d)`, `PumpAndSettle`, `Tap`, `LongPress`, `Drag`, `Fling`, `Hover`, `Scroll`, `EnterText`, `SendKeyEvent`, `SendKeyDownEvent`/`UpEvent`, `PumpFrames`
  - finders: `ByText`, `ByKey`, `ByType[T]`, `ByIcon`, `ByTooltip`, `BySemanticsLabel`, `Descendant`, `Ancestor`
  - assertions are plain functions returning errors for `t.Error` (`FindsOneWidget`, `FindsNothing`, ...); `MatchesGolden(name)` compares the view with a golden
  - a fake clock drives tickers and timers
  - the `fake` platform scripts windows, input and settings
- **Golden tests** compare against PNGs in `testdata/goldens/`, rendered by `internal/gpu/soft`. `-update-goldens` rewrites them.
- **Ported Flutter tests**: for each widget and render object, the relevant cases from Flutter's `packages/flutter/test` are ported as table tests. This is the main way to check fidelity.
- **Platform tests** (`-tags glutter_display`) open real Wayland/X11 windows and GL contexts (they can run under a headless compositor such as `weston --backend=headless` or `Xvfb`), and compare `gles` against `soft`.
- **Wayland compatibility matrix.**
  - Every milestone that touches the platform is checked against:
    - compositors: GNOME/Mutter, KDE/KWin, Sway, Hyprland, Weston and COSMIC
    - drivers: Mesa (Intel, AMD), NVIDIA proprietary, and llvmpipe
    - scales: 100%, 125%, 150% and 200%
    - a two-monitor setup with mixed scales
  - The automated part runs under headless compositors (`weston --backend=headless`, `sway` with `WLR_BACKENDS=headless`, `mutter --headless`, `kwin_wayland --virtual`).
  - The rest is a manual checklist in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` covering every item in §8.11.
- **D-Bus services** are tested against an in-process fake bus that records method calls and emits signals. Real-desktop checks are listed per milestone in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md`.
- **Both build modes**:
  - `go test -race ./...` runs with cgo (purego uses the real cgo runtime)
  - `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./...` runs purego's cgo-free path, which users get
  - both must pass
- **C struct layout tests** for every Go mirror of a C struct (§8.1 rule 3).
- **Fuzz tests** cover the X11/Wayland event decoders, the XKB compose handling, the paragraph layout, and the image and font loaders.

## 18. Milestones

Each milestone is broken into steps in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` (written after this spec is approved) and follows the workflow in CLAUDE.md.

| # | Milestone | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | Skeleton: `go.mod`, package layout, `cmd/glutter-gen` stub, `reference/` fetch script, `third_party/flutter/LICENSE` | `go build ./...` and all four checks pass |
| M1 | Platform core: purego library loaders and C struct mirrors, Wayland binding generator, Wayland and X11 window, event loop, EGL, GLES clear, frame callbacks, pointer and keyboard (xkb), fake platform | a window opens and closes and logs input on both backends |
| M2 | `ui` engine + software renderer: geometry, Path, Paint, Canvas, Picture, SceneBuilder, analytic shapes, shadows, gradients, clips, layers, golden harness | goldens for every `Canvas` operation |
| M3 | GLES renderer to parity with `soft`, plus atlases and batching | `gles` matches the `soft` goldens within tolerance |
| M4 | Text: font collection, fontscan, Roboto embedded, Paragraph with every query, glyph atlas | text goldens, including bidi, ellipsis and decorations |
| M5 | foundation, scheduler, physics, gestures (hit testing, arena, every recognizer) | ported Flutter gesture tests pass |
| M6 | rendering core: RenderObject, RenderBox, PipelineOwner, layers, compositing, every box render object | ported Flutter rendering tests pass |
| M7 | widgets core: Element tree, State, keys, GlobalKey, InheritedWidget family, basic widgets, MediaQuery, `RunApp`, `gluttertest` | counter example runs on Linux |
| M8 | animation library, implicit and explicit animations, transitions, Hero | ported animation tests pass |
| M9 | scrolling: Scrollable, physics, ListView/GridView/CustomScrollView, slivers, PageView, NestedScrollView, scrollbars, reorderable lists | ported scrolling tests pass |
| M10 | navigation and focus: Navigator (both APIs), Router, Overlay, OverlayPortal, focus system, Shortcuts/Actions | ported tests pass |
| M11 | text editing: EditableText, selection, Linux shortcuts, undo, IME (text-input-v3 on Wayland; IBus/Fcitx5 over D-Bus on X11), clipboard, SelectableRegion | editing works with IBus and Fcitx5 on both backends |
| M12 | Material foundation: ThemeData, ColorScheme (+ HCT port), Typography, Material, Ink, icons, localizations | theme goldens |
| M13 | Material components, in batches: app structure → actions → inputs → dialogs and pickers → display | gallery example covers §11.2 |
| M14 | desktop windowing: multiple windows, dialog windows, decorations (server-side, libdecor, app-drawn), cursor shapes, fractional scale, settings, drag and drop, every item in §8.11 | multiwindow example; drag and drop with Files/Dolphin; the Wayland matrix (§17) passes |
| M15 | D-Bus services: tray, notifications, file dialogs, open URI | tray example on KDE and GNOME+AppIndicator |
| M16 | accessibility: semantics → AT-SPI2 | gallery usable with Orca |
| M17 | effects and performance: backdrop filter, advanced blend modes, raster cache, performance overlay, benchmarks meeting §15 | targets met |
| M18 | 1.0: documentation, examples, API review against §12 | release |

## 19. Examples (`examples/`)

`counter`, `layout` (Row/Column/Stack/Wrap), `animations`, `scrolling` (lists, grids, slivers), `navigation`, `editor` (text editing, clipboard, IME), `gallery` (all Material components, light/dark), `multiwindow`, `tray` (tray + notifications + file dialog), `custompaint`.

**Reference sources.** `reference/flutter` holds a sparse checkout of `packages/flutter/lib` and `packages/flutter/test`, pinned to the latest stable Flutter tag when M0 starts (the tag is recorded in `reference/VERSION`). It is git-ignored and fetched by `scripts/fetch-reference.sh`.

## 21. Open decisions

None at the moment. New ones are added here with a recommendation and moved to §4 once decided.

## 22. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Without cgo, purego imitates the cgo runtime (`internal/fakecgo`), which depends on Go runtime internals and can break with a new Go release | Pin the purego version. Run both test modes (§17) on every new Go release before raising the minimum Go version. Ebiten depends on the same mechanism, so breakage is found and fixed upstream quickly |
| A wrong Go mirror of a C struct corrupts memory silently | layout tests (§8.1 rule 3); fuzz tests on event decoding |
| The C→Go callback limit (~2,000 per process) | callbacks are created once at startup and dispatch by user data (§8.1 rule 2); a test counts them |
| C variadic APIs can't be called (XIM, some Xlib calls) | non-variadic variants; IME over D-Bus on X11 |
| purego callbacks can't take structs by value | none of the libraries used need that (checked per library in M1) |
| libdecor's GTK plugin loads GTK 3 into the process (more memory, slower first window) | load it only on compositors without server-side decorations; `DecorationsApp` skips it; measure against §15 in M14 |
